{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"a14f4507-95af-48ed-9c63-84981379f70f","agentId":"a8949544ab5f0d3b9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Read-only review, kaggriculture repo at /Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture. Do NOT edit, run anything that writes, or commit. You reviewed ROLE_TAPE earlier today; that verdict is saved at docs/fable_verdict_on_role_tape_2026-08-23.md and I acted on it.\n\nNew question, from Alex. Two parts.\n\nCONTEXT — what is running now. A PPO run `shaped` on the training box:\n  --shaping-coef 1.0 --value-margin 0.3 --n-phases 1 --lr 5e-5\n  --vocab data/il/bundle_vocab_teacher.json\n  --init (a DAgger clone checkpoint, 0.9993 action agreement with the tape agent)\n  --tape-frac 0.40 --opponent-frac 0.20 --farm-frac 0.10\nTerminal reward is win 1 / draw 0.5 / loss 0. `kagg/rl/net_worth.py` potential-based shaping was just switched on for the first time; it has been built and unused for weeks.\n\nMEASURED STATE of the policy before shaping was enabled, at update ~31:\n  vs tape 0.000 over 803 games, 0% draws.  vs farm 0.000/221.  vs pool 0.621/397 (64% draws).\n  bank median $0, max $85.\n  harvest/plant ratio 0.37 (plant 8.7% of ops, harvest 3.2%). The tape teacher's own ratio is 2.05.\n  hire fires on 52% of turns. It sells MORE often than the teacher but has nothing in the shed.\n  CLAUDE.md records harvest/plant correlating +0.439 with money and raw PLANT -0.358.\n\nQUESTION 1 — the scale. I measured `kagg/rl/money_potential.py`: FLOOR 0.0, SCALE 100000.0, EXPONENT 2.0, so PHI(m) = (m/100000)^2 clipped at 1.\n  $3,000 -> 0.0009    $10,000 -> 0.0100    $30,000 -> 0.0900\n  $50,000 -> 0.2500   $100,000 -> 1.0000\nAt our actual net worth (~$3,000), harvesting $250 of produce moves PHI by 0.00016 against a terminal win of 1.0 — one part in 6,000. Is `--shaping-coef 1.0` therefore doing essentially nothing where the policy actually lives, and if so what is the right response: raise coef, change EXPONENT/SCALE, use a different potential, or is the convexity load-bearing for a reason I am missing? Note money_potential.py says the convexity is Alex's explicit design (\"a $5 win over an equally broke mirror is worth almost nothing\") and that an Elo ladder does not score margin. Weigh that against the fact that a potential which is flat at the policy's current position cannot relocate credit there. Give numbers.\n\nQUESTION 2 — Alex's objection, verbatim: \"i think plant negative is crazy\" and \"no plant should be zero\". Under `net_worth.py` a PLANT spends seed cash while a standing crop is deliberately valued at zero (`standing_yield_value` values only `yield_units`, product that has already grown), so dPHI is negative. Meanwhile BUY_ANIMAL is a wash because livestock is valued at ANIMAL_COST. Same act — spend cash, acquire an asset that pays later — accounted two different ways. Is valuing a standing crop at its SEED COST (book cost, not expected yield) the correct fix, does it stay policy-invariant, and does it interact badly with anything: the FERTILIZE hole that `standing_yield_value` exists to close, weeds/crop death (a crop that dies should presumably write the asset off), or `max_lifespan_step`. Cite file:line. If it is the right fix, say what would have to be true for it to be measurably better, and name the cheapest experiment that would show it.\n\nBe blunt. Verdict in the first sentence. Do not be polite."},"uuid":"a7551885-b534-40fa-972b-6f5239957804","timestamp":"2026-08-23T19:03:55.451Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

This skill should be used when the user is looking for functionality that might exist as an installable skill.\n- humanizer: Remove signs of AI-generated writing from text. Uses az rest and curl against the Fabric REST API to author definitions containing base64-encoded mashup.pq, queryMetadata.json, and .platform parts. Use when the user wants to: (1) create a new Dataflow Gen2 with Power Query M queries, (2) update a dataflow mashup definition, (3) trigger a dataflow refresh job, (4) bind or manage dataflow connections, (5) set up CI/CD via definition export and import, (6) delete a dataflow, (7) configure staging destinations. Use when the user wants to: (1) list dataflows, (2) inspect a dataflow definition and decode its mashup, (3) discover parameters, (4) check refresh status, (5) retrieve job history, (6) analyze staging settings, (7) examine connections and data source bindings. Triggers: \"dataflow status\", \"refresh history\", \"dataflow monitor\", \"list dataflows\", \"dataflow parameters\", \"explore dataflow\", \"inspect dataflow\", \"dataflow run status\".\n- fabric-skills:dataflows-save-as-authoring-cli: Assess, plan, and execute dataflow Gen1 → Gen2.1 CI/CD save-as operations via CLI (az rest / curl) against both Power BI REST and Fabric REST APIs. Scan workspaces or entire tenants for Gen1 dataflows, evaluate save-as readiness with seven risk signals (incremental refresh, BYOSA storage, Power Automate triggers, pipeline dependencies, linked entities, DirectQuery, caller-not-owner), produce a Save-As Readiness Snapshot (markdown + JSON), and invoke the SaveAsNativeArtifact API to create upgraded Gen2.1 copies of Gen1 dataflows. Use when the user wants to: (1) discover Gen1 dataflows in a workspace or tenant, (2) assess save-as readiness and risk signals, (3) upgrade or migrate Gen1 into a Gen2.1 copy, (4) validate post-save-as data integrity, (5) detect residual Gen1 references. Triggers: \"save Gen1 dataflow\", \"convert dataflow Gen1\", \"upgrade dataflow\", \"migrate dataflow\", \"dataflow readiness\", \"Gen1 to Gen2\", \"dataflow save-as assessment\", \"saveAsNativeArtifact\", \"dataflow save-as scan\".\n- fabric-skills:e2e-medallion-architecture: Implement end-to-end Medallion Architecture (Bronze/Silver/Gold) lakehouse patterns in Microsoft Fabric using PySpark, Delta Lake, and Fabric Pipelines. {"parentUuid":"2ea9077d-6628-4b43-918e-91de5ac9719c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"a14f4507-95af-48ed-9c63-84981379f70f","agentId":"a8949544ab5f0d3b9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JpGAyJZQCuwsPihiVTQ8S7","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Optional potential-based money shaping: zero at the starting stake, convex above it.\n2\t\n3\t**Status: built, tested, and not wired into anything. `coef` defaults to 0.0, which is byte-exactly\n4\toff.** Whether to use it is an open decision -- see the trade-off at the bottom.\n5\t\n6\t**The idea is Alex's**, in three parts: put the zero at $3,000 so hoarding earns nothing, scale with\n7\tmoney above that, and make the scaling convex so \"$5 more than a broke mirror\" is worth almost\n8\tnothing while a real farm is worth a lot.\n9\t\n10\t**The form is what makes it safe.** Adding `f(final_bank)` to the terminal reward would change the\n11\toptimum to margin maximisation, which an Elo ladder does not score -- `CLAUDE.md`: *\"A $43k win and a\n12\t$4k win score identically.\"* Adding `F(s, s') = gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` per step instead leaves the\n13\toptimal policy **provably unchanged** (Ng, Harada & Russell 1999). Its entire effect is to relocate\n14\tcredit in time, which is the actual complaint rather than a wish about the objective.\n15\t\n16\t**The problem it addresses.** To plant a melon the policy spends seed money now and sells the crop\n17\t~240 turns later. With `--horizon 32` and `--gae-lambda 0.95` the credit window is ~20 turns, so the\n18\t*cost* lands inside it and the *benefit* lands far outside. The policy experiences the spend and\n19\tnever the payoff, which is consistent with everything measured: it ends every season on $0\n20\t(`docs/bankrupt_every_game_2026-08-03.md`) while HARVEST runs 0.18-0.24% of ops. Per-step differences\n21\tput both halves on the turns they happen.\n22\t\n23\t**Why it is not simply the per-step bank delta** that `CLAUDE.md` marks *\"NO LONGER IN FORCE\"*. That\n24\twas `PHI = money` -- linear, unfloored, and therefore paying for thrift, since clawing back the stake\n25\traises it. Flooring at the opening stake makes the whole $0-$3,000 region flat, so going broke is not\n26\tpunished and hoarding is not rewarded; only *earning* moves it. The convex exponent then does the\n27\trest of Alex's requirement.\n28\t\n29\t**It composes with P2b rather than replacing it** (`kagg/rl/opportunity.py`). P2b resumes the episode\n30\tnext to a ripening crop, but the episode still runs to day 30, so credit from the harvest to the\n31\tterminal win still has to flow through the critic -- the one component this project has not verified.\n32\tThis term makes the harvest pay on its own turn without depending on the critic at all.\n33\t\n34\t**The trade-off, stated so it can be weighed rather than assumed.** For: bounded downside, since\n35\tpolicy-invariance is a theorem and not a judgement call; and it does not need the critic to work.\n36\tAgainst: one more hyperparameter (`coef`) that nobody can set a priori, and switching it on mid-stream\n37\tmakes runs incomparable. The natural decision point is the 30M-turn read -- if `bank_final_med` is\n38\tstill $0 then, the critic is not carrying credit and this is the cheapest thing that does not rely on\n39\tit.\n40\t\"\"\"\n41\t\n42\timport jax.numpy as jnp\n43\t\n44\t# **Was $3,000, the opening stake. Moved to 0 on 2026-08-03, and the reason is measured.**\n45\t#\n46\t# The floor existed so hoarding earns nothing: clawing the stake back is thrift, not production. That\n47\t# reason is right and the mechanism was wrong, because the **convexity already does it**, far more\n48\t# sharply than a hard floor. At `EXPONENT = 2` a $3,000 bank is worth 1.1% of a $28,168 season and a\n49\t# $1,856 bank is worth 0.4%. Thrift is already worthless.\n50\t#\n51\t# What the hard floor added was a **dead zone**. `day0-long-20260803-200350` saw `bank_final_med` fall\n52\t# $20,655 -> $1,856 over 300 updates, so the policy spends whole seasons entirely below $3,000 where\n53\t# `PHI` is identically zero -- no gradient in precisely the region that needs one. Alex's requirement\n54\t# was \"0 for 0, much much more for 100k\", and `FLOOR = 0` satisfies it literally.\n55\tFLOOR = 0.0\n56\t\n57\t# **The cap.** `PHI` was unbounded, so at $110,000 it read 1.21 and at `--shaping-coef 6` contributed\n58\t# 7.3 against a terminal reward of 1.0. Invariance does not protect against that: Ng's theorem fixes\n59\t# the optimum, not what PPO does with a term that swamps the objective, and the sigmoid value head\n60\t# cannot represent it either. `SCALE` was already chosen so a top-of-leaderboard season lands near 1.0,\n61\t# so the cap binds nowhere we can currently reach and makes `--value-margin` computable rather than\n62\t# guessed. Clipping is safe: `F = gamma*PHI(s') - PHI(s)` telescopes for *any* PHI.\n63\tCAP = 1.0\n64\t\n65\t# $100,000 above the stake, close to the $106k and $113k the top two agents banked in episode\n66\t# 89197802 (`CLAUDE.md`). Normalising there puts a realistic winning season at PHI ~ 1, the same\n67\t# order as the terminal reward, rather than dwarfing it.\n68\tSCALE = 100_000.0\n69\t\n70\t# > 1 makes it convex: the same $1,000 is worth far more at $90k than at $4k, which is what\n71\t# separates a real farm from a $5 win against an equally broke mirror.\n72\tEXPONENT = 2.0\n73\t\n74\t# **Off.** A run with this at 0 is byte-identical to a run made before the term existed.\n75\tDEFAULT_COEF = 0.0\n76\t\n77\t\n78\tdef potential(money, floor=FLOOR, scale=SCALE, k=EXPONENT):\n79\t    \"\"\"`PHI(s)` -- zero at or below `floor`, convex above it, reaching 1.0 at `floor + scale`.\n80\t\n81\t    Clipped rather than signed. A signed potential would pay the policy for clawing its stake back\n82\t    toward $3,000, and recovering the stake is thrift, not production -- the incentive Alex objected\n83\t    to in the graded-opponent proposal and the reason that proposal was dropped below $3,000.\n84\t    \"\"\"\n85\t    above = jnp.maximum(jnp.asarray(money, jnp.float32) - floor, 0.0)\n86\t    return jnp.minimum((above / scale) ** k, CAP)\n87\t\n88\t\n89\tdef shaping(money_before, money_after, coef=DEFAULT_COEF, **kw):\n90\t    \"\"\"`PHI(after) - PHI(before)`, elementwise, preserving shape.\n91\t\n92\t    `gamma` is 1.0 throughout this project (`CLAUDE.md`, and `--gamma-per-day 1.0` on every current\n93\t    run), so the discount factor in `gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` is the identity and is omitted rather\n94\t    than threaded through. If a discount ever returns, it belongs here.\n95\t\n96\t    The terms telescope, so an episode's total shaping is `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` whatever path was\n97\t    taken -- which is what makes it unable to pay for a detour, and is checked directly in\n98\t    `test_the_shaping_telescopes_so_only_the_endpoints_matter`.\n99\t    \"\"\"\n100\t    return coef * (potential(money_after, **kw) - potential(money_before, **kw))\n101\t"}]},"uuid":"15bafec2-3aa5-46be-b8f2-45c5022908d5","timestamp":"2026-08-23T19:04:02.213Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/kagg/rl/money_potential.py","content":"\"\"\"Optional potential-based money shaping: zero at the starting stake, convex above it.\n\n**Status: built, tested, and not wired into anything. `coef` defaults to 0.0, which is byte-exactly\noff.** Whether to use it is an open decision -- see the trade-off at the bottom.\n\n**The idea is Alex's**, in three parts: put the zero at $3,000 so hoarding earns nothing, scale with\nmoney above that, and make the scaling convex so \"$5 more than a broke mirror\" is worth almost\nnothing while a real farm is worth a lot.\n\n**The form is what makes it safe.** Adding `f(final_bank)` to the terminal reward would change the\noptimum to margin maximisation, which an Elo ladder does not score -- `CLAUDE.md`: *\"A $43k win and a\n$4k win score identically.\"* Adding `F(s, s') = gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` per step instead leaves the\noptimal policy **provably unchanged** (Ng, Harada & Russell 1999). Its entire effect is to relocate\ncredit in time, which is the actual complaint rather than a wish about the objective.\n\n**The problem it addresses.** To plant a melon the policy spends seed money now and sells the crop\n~240 turns later. With `--horizon 32` and `--gae-lambda 0.95` the credit window is ~20 turns, so the\n*cost* lands inside it and the *benefit* lands far outside. The policy experiences the spend and\nnever the payoff, which is consistent with everything measured: it ends every season on $0\n(`docs/bankrupt_every_game_2026-08-03.md`) while HARVEST runs 0.18-0.24% of ops. Per-step differences\nput both halves on the turns they happen.\n\n**Why it is not simply the per-step bank delta** that `CLAUDE.md` marks *\"NO LONGER IN FORCE\"*. That\nwas `PHI = money` -- linear, unfloored, and therefore paying for thrift, since clawing back the stake\nraises it. Flooring at the opening stake makes the whole $0-$3,000 region flat, so going broke is not\npunished and hoarding is not rewarded; only *earning* moves it. The convex exponent then does the\nrest of Alex's requirement.\n\n**It composes with P2b rather than replacing it** (`kagg/rl/opportunity.py`). P2b resumes the episode\nnext to a ripening crop, but the episode still runs to day 30, so credit from the harvest to the\nterminal win still has to flow through the critic -- the one component this project has not verified.\nThis term makes the harvest pay on its own turn without depending on the critic at all.\n\n**The trade-off, stated so it can be weighed rather than assumed.** For: bounded downside, since\npolicy-invariance is a theorem and not a judgement call; and it does not need the critic to work.\nAgainst: one more hyperparameter (`coef`) that nobody can set a priori, and switching it on mid-stream\nmakes runs incomparable. The natural decision point is the 30M-turn read -- if `bank_final_med` is\nstill $0 then, the critic is not carrying credit and this is the cheapest thing that does not rely on\nit.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\n\n# **Was $3,000, the opening stake. Moved to 0 on 2026-08-03, and the reason is measured.**\n#\n# The floor existed so hoarding earns nothing: clawing the stake back is thrift, not production. That\n# reason is right and the mechanism was wrong, because the **convexity already does it**, far more\n# sharply than a hard floor. At `EXPONENT = 2` a $3,000 bank is worth 1.1% of a $28,168 season and a\n# $1,856 bank is worth 0.4%. Thrift is already worthless.\n#\n# What the hard floor added was a **dead zone**. `day0-long-20260803-200350` saw `bank_final_med` fall\n# $20,655 -> $1,856 over 300 updates, so the policy spends whole seasons entirely below $3,000 where\n# `PHI` is identically zero -- no gradient in precisely the region that needs one. Alex's requirement\n# was \"0 for 0, much much more for 100k\", and `FLOOR = 0` satisfies it literally.\nFLOOR = 0.0\n\n# **The cap.** `PHI` was unbounded, so at $110,000 it read 1.21 and at `--shaping-coef 6` contributed\n# 7.3 against a terminal reward of 1.0. Invariance does not protect against that: Ng's theorem fixes\n# the optimum, not what PPO does with a term that swamps the objective, and the sigmoid value head\n# cannot represent it either. `SCALE` was already chosen so a top-of-leaderboard season lands near 1.0,\n# so the cap binds nowhere we can currently reach and makes `--value-margin` computable rather than\n# guessed. Clipping is safe: `F = gamma*PHI(s') - PHI(s)` telescopes for *any* PHI.\nCAP = 1.0\n\n# $100,000 above the stake, close to the $106k and $113k the top two agents banked in episode\n# 89197802 (`CLAUDE.md`). Normalising there puts a realistic winning season at PHI ~ 1, the same\n# order as the terminal reward, rather than dwarfing it.\nSCALE = 100_000.0\n\n# > 1 makes it convex: the same $1,000 is worth far more at $90k than at $4k, which is what\n# separates a real farm from a $5 win against an equally broke mirror.\nEXPONENT = 2.0\n\n# **Off.** A run with this at 0 is byte-identical to a run made before the term existed.\nDEFAULT_COEF = 0.0\n\n\ndef potential(money, floor=FLOOR, scale=SCALE, k=EXPONENT):\n    \"\"\"`PHI(s)` -- zero at or below `floor`, convex above it, reaching 1.0 at `floor + scale`.\n\n    Clipped rather than signed. A signed potential would pay the policy for clawing its stake back\n    toward $3,000, and recovering the stake is thrift, not production -- the incentive Alex objected\n    to in the graded-opponent proposal and the reason that proposal was dropped below $3,000.\n    \"\"\"\n    above = jnp.maximum(jnp.asarray(money, jnp.float32) - floor, 0.0)\n    return jnp.minimum((above / scale) ** k, CAP)\n\n\ndef shaping(money_before, money_after, coef=DEFAULT_COEF, **kw):\n    \"\"\"`PHI(after) - PHI(before)`, elementwise, preserving shape.\n\n    `gamma` is 1.0 throughout this project (`CLAUDE.md`, and `--gamma-per-day 1.0` on every current\n    run), so the discount factor in `gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` is the identity and is omitted rather\n    than threaded through. If a discount ever returns, it belongs here.\n\n    The terms telescope, so an episode's total shaping is `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` whatever path was\n    taken -- which is what makes it unable to pay for a detour, and is checked directly in\n    `test_the_shaping_telescopes_so_only_the_endpoints_matter`.\n    \"\"\"\n    return coef * (potential(money_after, **kw) - potential(money_before, **kw))\n","numLines":101,"startLine":1,"totalLines":101}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2ea9077d-6628-4b43-918e-91de5ac9719c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

{"parentUuid":"674aa94c-1809-4c42-bc78-a1ff1733f047","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"a14f4507-95af-48ed-9c63-84981379f70f","agentId":"a8949544ab5f0d3b9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NsAxVrZUuVsvh5XR8JDNuG","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"What a farm is worth marked to market, and the potential built on it.\n2\t\n3\t**Why this exists rather than `money_potential` alone.** That one values *cash*, so it punishes the\n4\tanimal chain at the moment of investment: buying a goose moves money $12,000 -> $11,600 and the milk\n5\tarrives 100+ turns later. It makes the *cost* of the chain more visible while leaving the benefit\n6\texactly as far away, which is the opposite of the intent. Alex's objection, and it is correct -- a\n7\tgoose in a pasture is **capital**, and cash accounting books it as pure loss until it produces.\n8\t\n9\tValuing assets makes a purchase at fair value a **wash**: cash down $400, livestock up $400, `PHI`\n10\tunchanged. That single property is what makes this accounting rather than strategy. It says assets are\n11\tworth what they would fetch; it does not say animals are good. A `PHI` that *rewarded* buying an\n12\tanimal would be encoding our belief, and while it would still be policy-invariant -- any potential\n13\ttelescopes -- it would bias finite-sample search toward that belief.\n14\t\n15\t**What it does and does not buy.** It removes the penalty on the chain's head; it does not create a\n16\tpull up it. Under fair-value accounting every pure conversion is `dPHI = 0`:\n17\t\n18\t    BUILD_PASTURE   free, worthless            dPHI = 0\n19\t    BUY_ANIMAL      cash -$400, asset +$400    dPHI = 0\n20\t    PICKUP / PLACE  nothing moves              dPHI = 0\n21\t    FEED            -wheat, +restored slack    dPHI > 0\n22\t    HARVEST         product appears            dPHI > 0\n23\t\n24\tSo the head is a **plateau**, not a hill. That converts a 600-turn credit desert into a ~4-action\n25\tplateau, which is a real gain -- the tail now pays on the turn it happens without depending on a critic\n26\tsitting at `ev_signal` 0.30 -- but entropy is still the only thing crossing it. The reason to cross has\n27\tto come from an opponent that melon cannot beat. See `docs/ideas.md`.\n28\t\n29\t**The trap, and why FEED is positive here.** Naive book-cost accounting gets the sign backwards on the\n30\ttwo ops that matter most: `FEED` destroys a unit of wheat and `FERTILIZE` destroys a unit of\n31\tfertiliser, and neither adds anything to a balance sheet. Under a naive net worth both are negative --\n32\tit would discourage the strongest money predictors in the game (FEED +0.452, CARE +0.441 over 312 real\n33\tseats, `CLAUDE.md`).\n34\t\n35\tThe fix is to value the intangible, on a basis the simulator supplies rather than one we choose:\n36\t`jax_sim.py:1147-1159` escapes an animal at `consecutive_unfed >= 2`, so an animal one night from\n37\tbolting really is worth less than a fed one. Feeding restores that slack, and the arithmetic\n38\t($50 of wheat against half of a $300-$500 animal) makes FEED strongly positive.\n39\t\n40\t**The daily oscillation is signal, not noise.** Because freshness is computed from what\n41\t`consecutive_unfed` *will* be tonight, a fed animal marks at 1.0 and drops to 0.5 after the nightly\n42\tpass -- so an animal fed every day oscillates. That looked like noise worth avoiding, but it is not:\n43\tit is a **daily rent**. Feed and you take the gain; skip and you take only the loss. Over a season the\n44\tterms telescope to `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` regardless, so nothing is being invented.\n45\t\n46\t**Standing crops are deliberately NOT valued.** Expected yield x price x maturity is the most\n47\tjudgement-laden piece and the easiest place to smuggle in a strategy. v1 leaves it out; a harvest\n48\tshows up as inventory the moment it lands, which is one link later but still ~700 turns earlier than\n49\ttoday.\n50\t\n51\t**Known conservatism.** Held product is marked at the town's current price, but selling N units moves\n52\tthat price down (`price(inventory)`), so a large holding is marked optimistically. A first-order mark\n53\tis the sim's own number and is the honest default; it is recorded here so nobody reads the figure as a\n54\tliquidation value.\n55\t\"\"\"\n56\t\n57\timport jax.numpy as jnp\n58\t\n59\tfrom kagg.sim import jax_sim as J\n60\t\n61\t# Re-exported so there is exactly one convex-potential formula in the project. `money_potential`\n62\t# documents the choice of each constant: zero at the $3,000 opening stake so hoarding earns nothing,\n63\t# normalised at $100,000 so a realistic winning season sits at PHI ~ 1 rather than dwarfing the\n64\t# terminal reward, and convex so a $5 win over an equally broke mirror is worth almost nothing.\n65\tfrom kagg.rl.money_potential import (  # noqa: F401\n66\t    DEFAULT_COEF, EXPONENT, FLOOR, SCALE, potential, shaping,\n67\t)\n68\t\n69\t# `jax_sim.py:1159`: `escaped = has_animal & (unfed >= 2)`. Not a tunable -- it is the rule.\n70\tESCAPE_AFTER = 2.0\n71\t\n72\t\n73\tdef freshness(tiles):\n74\t    \"\"\"`[B, P, TILES]` in [0, 1]: how much of an animal's value survives tonight.\n75\t\n76\t    Computed from what `consecutive_unfed` **will be** after the nightly pass, not what it is now, so\n77\t    the credit for a FEED lands on the turn the unit feeds rather than hours later at the day boundary.\n78\t    An animal already at `unfed == 1` and unfed again tonight escapes, so it marks at zero -- which is\n79\t    correct, and is what makes skipping a feed cost something immediately.\n80\t    \"\"\"\n81\t    tonight = jnp.where(tiles.fed_today, 0, tiles.consecutive_unfed + 1).astype(jnp.float32)\n82\t    return jnp.clip(1.0 - tonight / ESCAPE_AFTER, 0.0, 1.0)\n83\t\n84\t\n85\tdef livestock_value(tiles):\n86\t    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- placed animals at cost, discounted by how close they are to escaping.\"\"\"\n87\t    has_animal = tiles.animal != J.NO_ANIMAL\n88\t    cost = J.ANIMAL_COST[jnp.clip(tiles.animal, 0, J.ANIMAL_COST.shape[0] - 1)]\n89\t    return jnp.where(has_animal, cost.astype(jnp.float32) * freshness(tiles), 0.0).sum(axis=-1)\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef stock_value(state):\n93\t    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- everything in the shed and in units' hands, marked to market.\n94\t\n95\t    The item axis is `PRODUCTS + ANIMALS` (`market.py:42`), so the split below is a slice. That layout\n96\t    is pinned by `tests/test_net_worth.py::test_animals_occupy_the_tail_of_the_item_axis` rather than\n97\t    assumed: an item inserted in the middle would silently price livestock as a product.\n98\t\n99\t    An animal in the shed is not yet on a feeding clock, so it marks at full cost -- which is what\n100\t    makes buying one a wash.\n101\t    \"\"\"\n102\t    held = (state.farm.shed + state.farm.carried.sum(axis=2)).astype(jnp.float32)\n103\t    px = J.price(state.inventory).astype(jnp.float32)              # [B, N_PRODUCTS]\n104\t    products = (held[..., :J.N_PRODUCTS] * px[:, None, :]).sum(axis=-1)\n105\t    animals = (held[..., J.N_PRODUCTS:] * J.ANIMAL_COST[None, None, :].astype(jnp.float32)\n106\t               ).sum(axis=-1)\n107\t    return products + animals\n108\t\n109\t\n110\tdef standing_yield_value(state):\n111\t    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- product that has grown but not yet been harvested, marked to market.\n112\t\n113\t    **This is the fix for the FERTILIZE hole, and it is load-bearing.** Without it `FERTILIZE` is\n114\t    strictly negative: it destroys a ~$100 unit of fertiliser and, with nothing on the plant side of\n115\t    the ledger, adds nothing. So v1 would have *discouraged* the op that the replay measures at\n116\t    exactly `raw 0` -- the precise trap this module's own docstring was written to warn about, and one\n117\t    that only FEED had a test for.\n118\t\n119\t    Unpicked yield genuinely is an asset: `jax_sim.py:1048` decrements `yield_units` when a unit\n120\t    harvests it, so it is product that exists and is waiting to be collected. Valuing it means a\n121\t    doubled melon yield shows up ~a day after fertilising rather than never, which puts the credit\n122\t    ~24 turns out instead of ~700.\n123\t\n124\t    It stops short of valuing *future* yield -- expected units x price x maturity -- which is where the\n125\t    judgement lives and where a strategy could be smuggled in. A plant that will produce tomorrow is\n126\t    worth nothing here until it does.\n127\t\n128\t    `ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT` and `CROP_TO_PRODUCT` are the simulator's own tables (`jax_sim.py:169`,\n129\t    `tiles.py:61`), so the pairing is not something this file decides.\n130\t    \"\"\"\n131\t    t = state.tiles\n132\t    px = J.price(state.inventory).astype(jnp.float32)                  # [B, N_PRODUCTS]\n133\t    has_animal = t.animal != J.NO_ANIMAL\n134\t    crop_product = J.CROP_TO_PRODUCT[jnp.clip(t.crop, 0, J.CROP_TO_PRODUCT.shape[0] - 1)]\n135\t    animal_product = J.ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT[jnp.clip(t.animal, 0, J.ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT.shape[0] - 1)]\n136\t    product = jnp.where(has_animal, animal_product, crop_product)      # [B, P, TILES]\n137\t\n138\t    wide = jnp.broadcast_to(px[:, None, :], product.shape[:-1] + (px.shape[-1],))\n139\t    unit_price = jnp.take_along_axis(wide, product, axis=-1)\n140\t    return (t.yield_units.astype(jnp.float32) * unit_price).sum(axis=-1)\n141\t\n142\t\n143\tdef net_worth(state):\n144\t    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- cash plus livestock plus stock plus unharvested yield.\n145\t\n146\t    Structures are free to build (`jax_sim.py:613`), so they are worth nothing -- which is why the\n147\t    chain's head is a plateau rather than a hill. See the module docstring.\n148\t    \"\"\"\n149\t    return (state.farm.money.astype(jnp.float32)\n150\t            + livestock_value(state.tiles)\n151\t            + stock_value(state)\n152\t            + standing_yield_value(state))\n153\t\n154\t\n155\tdef net_worth_shaping(before, after, coef=DEFAULT_COEF):\n156\t    \"\"\"`coef * (PHI(net_worth(after)) - PHI(net_worth(before)))`, shaped `[B, P]`.\n157\t\n158\t    `gamma` is 1.0 throughout this project, so the discount in `gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` is the\n159\t    identity and is omitted. The terms telescope, so a season's total shaping is\n160\t    `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` whatever route was taken -- it cannot pay for a detour.\n161\t    \"\"\"\n162\t    return shaping(net_worth(before), net_worth(after), coef=coef)\n163\t"}]},"uuid":"3364bb3f-4550-44f7-9e10-d13908e50c6c","timestamp":"2026-08-23T19:04:02.247Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/kagg/rl/net_worth.py","content":"\"\"\"What a farm is worth marked to market, and the potential built on it.\n\n**Why this exists rather than `money_potential` alone.** That one values *cash*, so it punishes the\nanimal chain at the moment of investment: buying a goose moves money $12,000 -> $11,600 and the milk\narrives 100+ turns later. It makes the *cost* of the chain more visible while leaving the benefit\nexactly as far away, which is the opposite of the intent. Alex's objection, and it is correct -- a\ngoose in a pasture is **capital**, and cash accounting books it as pure loss until it produces.\n\nValuing assets makes a purchase at fair value a **wash**: cash down $400, livestock up $400, `PHI`\nunchanged. That single property is what makes this accounting rather than strategy. It says assets are\nworth what they would fetch; it does not say animals are good. A `PHI` that *rewarded* buying an\nanimal would be encoding our belief, and while it would still be policy-invariant -- any potential\ntelescopes -- it would bias finite-sample search toward that belief.\n\n**What it does and does not buy.** It removes the penalty on the chain's head; it does not create a\npull up it. Under fair-value accounting every pure conversion is `dPHI = 0`:\n\n    BUILD_PASTURE   free, worthless            dPHI = 0\n    BUY_ANIMAL      cash -$400, asset +$400    dPHI = 0\n    PICKUP / PLACE  nothing moves              dPHI = 0\n    FEED            -wheat, +restored slack    dPHI > 0\n    HARVEST         product appears            dPHI > 0\n\nSo the head is a **plateau**, not a hill. See `docs/ideas.md`.\n\n**The trap, and why FEED is positive here.** Naive book-cost accounting gets the sign backwards on the\ntwo ops that matter most: `FEED` destroys a unit of wheat and `FERTILIZE` destroys a unit of\nfertiliser, and neither adds anything to a balance sheet. Under a naive net worth both are negative --\nit would discourage the strongest money predictors in the game (FEED +0.452, CARE +0.441 over 312 real\nseats, `CLAUDE.md`).\n\nThe fix is to value the intangible, on a basis the simulator supplies rather than one we choose:\n`jax_sim.py:1147-1159` escapes an animal at `consecutive_unfed >= 2`, so an animal one night from\nbolting really is worth less than a fed one. Feeding restores that slack, and the arithmetic\n($50 of wheat against half of a $300-$500 animal) makes FEED strongly positive.\n\n**The daily oscillation is signal, not noise.** Because freshness is computed from what\n`consecutive_unfed` *will* be tonight, a fed animal marks at 1.0 and drops to 0.5 after the nightly\npass -- so an animal fed every day oscillates. That looked like noise worth avoiding, but it is not:\nit is a **daily rent**. Feed and you take the gain; skip and you take only the loss. Over a season the\nterms telescope to `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` regardless, so nothing is being invented.\n\n**Standing crops are deliberately NOT valued.** Expected yield x price x maturity is the most\njudgement-laden piece and the easiest place to smuggle in a strategy. v1 leaves it out; a harvest\nshows up as inventory the moment it lands, which is one link later but still ~700 turns earlier than\ntoday.\n\n**Known conservatism.** Held product is marked at the town's current price, but selling N units moves\nthat price down (`price(inventory)`), so a large holding is marked optimistically. A first-order mark\nis the sim's own number and is the honest default; it is recorded here so nobody reads the figure as a\nliquidation value.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\n\nfrom kagg.sim import jax_sim as J\n\n# Re-exported so there is exactly one convex-potential formula in the project. `money_potential`\n# documents the choice of each constant: zero at the $3,000 opening stake so hoarding earns nothing,\n# normalised at $100,000 so a realistic winning season sits at PHI ~ 1 rather than dwarfing the\n# terminal reward, and convex so a $5 win over an equally broke mirror is worth almost nothing.\nfrom kagg.rl.money_potential import (  # noqa: F401\n    DEFAULT_COEF, EXPONENT, FLOOR, SCALE, potential, shaping,\n)\n\n# `jax_sim.py:1159`: `escaped = has_animal & (unfed >= 2)`. Not a tunable -- it is the rule.\nESCAPE_AFTER = 2.0\n\n\ndef freshness(tiles):\n    \"\"\"`[B, P, TILES]` in [0, 1]: how much of an animal's value survives tonight.\n\n    Computed from what `consecutive_unfed` **will be** after the nightly pass, not what it is now, so\n    the credit for a FEED lands on the turn the unit feeds rather than hours later at the day boundary.\n    An animal already at `unfed == 1` and unfed again tonight escapes, so it marks at zero -- which is\n    correct, and is what makes skipping a feed cost something immediately.\n    \"\"\"\n    tonight = jnp.where(tiles.fed_today, 0, tiles.consecutive_unfed + 1).astype(jnp.float32)\n    return jnp.clip(1.0 - tonight / ESCAPE_AFTER, 0.0, 1.0)\n\n\ndef livestock_value(tiles):\n    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- placed animals at cost, discounted by how close they are to escaping.\"\"\"\n    has_animal = tiles.animal != J.NO_ANIMAL\n    cost = J.ANIMAL_COST[jnp.clip(tiles.animal, 0, J.ANIMAL_COST.shape[0] - 1)]\n    return jnp.where(has_animal, cost.astype(jnp.float32) * freshness(tiles), 0.0).sum(axis=-1)\n\n\ndef stock_value(state):\n    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- everything in the shed and in units' hands, marked to market.\n\n    The item axis is `PRODUCTS + ANIMALS` (`market.py:42`), so the split below is a slice. That layout\n    is pinned by `tests/test_net_worth.py::test_animals_occupy_the_tail_of_the_item_axis` rather than\n    assumed: an item inserted in the middle would silently price livestock as a product.\n\n    An animal in the shed is not yet on a feeding clock, so it marks at full cost -- which is what\n    makes buying one a wash.\n    \"\"\"\n    held = (state.farm.shed + state.farm.carried.sum(axis=2)).astype(jnp.float32)\n    px = J.price(state.inventory).astype(jnp.float32)              # [B, N_PRODUCTS]\n    products = (held[..., :J.N_PRODUCTS] * px[:, None, :]).sum(axis=-1)\n    animals = (held[..., J.N_PRODUCTS:] * J.ANIMAL_COST[None, None, :].astype(jnp.float32)\n               ).sum(axis=-1)\n    return products + animals\n\n\ndef standing_yield_value(state):\n    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- product that has grown but not yet been harvested, marked to market.\n\n    **This is the fix for the FERTILIZE hole, and it is load-bearing.** Without it `FERTILIZE` is\n    strictly negative: it destroys a ~$100 unit of fertiliser and, with nothing on the plant side of\n    the ledger, adds nothing. So v1 would have *discouraged* the op that the replay measures at\n    exactly `raw 0` -- the precise trap this module's own docstring was written to warn about, and one\n    that only FEED had a test for.\n\n    Unpicked yield genuinely is an asset: `jax_sim.py:1048` decrements `yield_units` when a unit\n    harvests it, so it is product that exists and is waiting to be collected. Valuing it means a\n    doubled melon yield shows up ~a day after fertilising rather than never, which puts the credit\n    ~24 turns out instead of ~700.\n\n    It stops short of valuing *future* yield -- expected units x price x maturity -- which is where the\n    judgement lives and where a strategy could be smuggled in. A plant that will produce tomorrow is\n    worth nothing here until it does.\n\n    `ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT` and `CROP_TO_PRODUCT` are the simulator's own tables (`jax_sim.py:169`,\n    `tiles.py:61`), so the pairing is not something this file decides.\n    \"\"\"\n    t = state.tiles\n    px = J.price(state.inventory).astype(jnp.float32)                  # [B, N_PRODUCTS]\n    has_animal = t.animal != J.NO_ANIMAL\n    crop_product = J.CROP_TO_PRODUCT[jnp.clip(t.crop, 0, J.CROP_TO_PRODUCT.shape[0] - 1)]\n    animal_product = J.ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT[jnp.clip(t.animal, 0, J.ANIMAL_TO_PRODUCT.shape[0] - 1)]\n    product = jnp.where(has_animal, animal_product, crop_product)      # [B, P, TILES]\n\n    wide = jnp.broadcast_to(px[:, None, :], product.shape[:-1] + (px.shape[-1],))\n    unit_price = jnp.take_along_axis(wide, product, axis=-1)\n    return (t.yield_units.astype(jnp.float32) * unit_price).sum(axis=-1)\n\n\ndef net_worth(state):\n    \"\"\"`[B, P]` -- cash plus livestock plus stock plus unharvested yield.\n\n    Structures are free to build (`jax_sim.py:613`), so they are worth nothing -- which is why the\n    chain's head is a plateau rather than a hill. See the module docstring.\n    \"\"\"\n    return (state.farm.money.astype(jnp.float32)\n            + livestock_value(state.tiles)\n            + stock_value(state)\n            + standing_yield_value(state))\n\n\ndef net_worth_shaping(before, after, coef=DEFAULT_COEF):\n    \"\"\"`coef * (PHI(net_worth(after)) - PHI(net_worth(before)))`, shaped `[B, P]`.\n\n    `gamma` is 1.0 throughout this project, so the discount in `gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` is the\n    identity and is omitted. The terms telescope, so a season's total shaping is\n    `PHI(end) - PHI(start)` whatever route was taken -- it cannot pay for a detour.\n    \"\"\"\n    return shaping(net_worth(before), net_worth(after), coef=coef)\n","numLines":163,"startLine":1,"totalLines":163}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"674aa94c-1809-4c42-bc78-a1ff1733f047","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

{"parentUuid":"005c02d4-60f5-4769-b224-ce7669728b95","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"a14f4507-95af-48ed-9c63-84981379f70f","agentId":"a8949544ab5f0d3b9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01F6jEQ9eLKtCzr3EMTdsqxj","type":"tool_result","content":"660\t            # A truncation is already handled correctly a few lines down -- no reward, bootstrap\n661\t            # kept. This is the same treatment for the same reason, so `virgin` tracks whether a\n662\t            # slot has yet completed a genuine reset-to-turn-0 episode.\n663\t            reward = jnp.where(\n664\t                (just_done & ~virgin)[:, None], won + 0.5 * tie, 0.0).astype(jnp.float32)\n665\t\n666\t            # **Potential-based shaping on net worth, filling the zeros the terminal reward leaves.**\n667\t            #\n668\t            # There is no new pathway here: `reward` is already a per-turn `[B, 2]` slot that happens\n669\t            # to hold 0.0 on 718 of 719 turns, and GAE already consumes it every turn. This fills it.\n670\t            #\n671\t            # `F(s, s') = gamma * PHI(s') - PHI(s)` leaves the optimal policy **provably** unchanged\n672\t            # (Ng, Harada & Russell 1999); at gamma 1.0 the terms telescope to `PHI(end) - PHI(start)`,\n673\t            # so it cannot pay for a detour. That is what makes it categorically different from the\n674\t            # per-step bank delta `CLAUDE.md` marks NO LONGER IN FORCE -- that one *was* the reward and\n675\t            # changed the objective to margin maximisation, which an Elo ladder does not score.\n676\t            #\n677\t            # Net worth rather than cash, because a cash potential punishes the animal chain at the\n678\t            # moment of investment -- see `kagg/rl/net_worth.py`. At `shaping_coef 0.0` this term is\n679\t            # byte-exactly zero and the run is identical to one made before it existed.\n680\t            #\n681\t            # **Suppressed on the terminal turn**, for the same reason the terminal reward is\n682\t            # suppressed on a virgin slot: `nxt` there is the *reset* board, so `PHI(after)` would be\n683\t            # the opening stake and the difference would book the whole season's net worth as a loss on\n684\t            # its last turn. The telescoping still holds within an episode; only the seam is dropped.\n685\t            if shaping_coef:\n686\t                shaped = net_worth.net_worth_shaping(state, nxt, coef=shaping_coef)\n687\t                reward = reward + jnp.where(just_done[:, None], 0.0, shaped).astype(jnp.float32)\n688\t\n689\t            # Restart inside the scan, per group. Resetting only in Python after the rollout lets\n690\t            # the simulator keep stepping past turn 719 into a season that does not exist, and\n691\t            # those invalid turns would still be trained on. The PRNG key carries across so a new\n692\t            # episode does not replay the old one's weed draws.\n693\t            # Only the per-game fields are selected. `key` and `unlock_day` are not per game --\n694\t            # the PRNG key is a single [2] array for the whole state -- so a blanket tree_map over\n695\t            # the State tries to broadcast a [B] mask against it and fails.\n696\t            # **What a restarting slot restarts INTO.** Without a curriculum this is `J.reset`,\n697\t            # an empty farm at turn 0. With one it is a real recorded board part-way through a\n698\t            # winner's season, and the difference is the whole experiment.\n699\t            #\n700\t            # It is passed in rather than built here because building it parses replay JSON on the\n701\t            # host, which cannot happen inside a jitted scan -- and because the schedule changes it\n702\t            # only every few hundred updates, so rebuilding per update would dominate the run.\n703\t            #\n704\t            # The reward is untouched: still win/lose at the end of the season. What changes is the\n705\t            # state distribution, not the signal. That is what makes it categorically different from the\n            # per-step bank delta `CLAUDE.md` marks NO LONGER IN FORCE -- that one *was* the reward and\n            # changed the objective to margin maximisation, which an Elo ladder does not score.\n            #\n            # Net worth rather than cash, because a cash potential punishes the animal chain at the\n            # moment of investment -- see `kagg/rl/net_worth.py`. At `shaping_coef 0.0` this term is\n            # byte-exactly zero and the run is identical to one made before it existed.\n            #\n            # **Suppressed on the terminal turn**, for the same reason the terminal reward is\n            # suppressed on a virgin slot: `nxt` there is the *reset* board, so `PHI(after)` would be\n            # the opening stake and the difference would book the whole season's net worth as a loss on\n            # its last turn. The telescoping still holds within an episode; only the seam is dropped.\n            if shaping_coef:\n                shaped = net_worth.net_worth_shaping(state, nxt, coef=shaping_coef)\n                reward = reward + jnp.where(just_done[:, None], 0.0, shaped).astype(jnp.float32)\n\n            # Restart inside the scan, per group. With one it is a real recorded board part-way through a\n            # winner's season, and the difference is the whole experiment.\n            #\n            # It is passed in rather than built here because building it parses replay JSON on the\n            # host, which cannot happen inside a jitted scan -- and because the schedule changes it\n            # only every few hundred updates, so rebuilding per update would dominate the run.\n            #\n            # The reward is untouched: still win/lose at the end of the season. Left unbounded it drifts straight past that: with a\n        # 64-turn horizon against 719-turn episodes, only about one update in eleven contains a\n        # terminal reward, and the other ten train the critic purely on its own predictions. That\n        # bootstrap has no unique fixed point at gamma ~ 1, and it was measured climbing to 1.315 --\n        # an impossible value -- while `explained_variance` still read +0.98, because a critic that\n        # is self-consistently wrong explains its own targets perfectly.\n        #\n        # A sigmoid makes the impossible unreachable, and costs nothing when the estimate is honest.\n        #\n        # **`value_margin` widens the guard instead of removing it.** With potential shaping on, a\n        # state's return is `terminal + coef * (PHI(end) - PHI(s))`, which can sit above 1 (a win with\n        # net worth still climbing) or below 0 (a loss with net worth falling from here). `ev_signal` restricts to rows where an episode\n        # actually ended, which is the only version that can fail.\n        ret_d = batch_data_full[\"returns\"]\n        val_d = flatten(traj[\"value\"])\n        ev_d = 1.0 - (ret_d - val_d).var() / jnp.maximum(ret_d.var(), 1e-8)\n\n        # **Shaping diagnostics.** `traj[\"reward\"]` holds terminal + shaping; the terminal part is\n        # non-zero only on `done` rows, so differencing gives the shaping contribution without\n        # carrying a second array through the scan.\n        _r = flatten(traj[\"reward\"])\n        _terminal_part = jnp.where(flatten(traj[\"done\"]), _r, 0.0)\n        _shape_part = _r - _terminal_part\n        shape_abs_mean = jnp.abs(_shape_part).mean()\n        shape_share = jnp.abs(_shape_part).sum() / jnp.maximum(jnp.abs(_r).sum(), 1e-8)\n        # The critic's representable range. `rescale_value` maps a sigmoid onto [-margin, 1+margin],\n        # so a return outside that band cannot be predicted and the value loss is bounded below by\n        # the overshoot -- while `ev` stays high, because the predictions remain self-consistent.\n        _lo, _hi = -args.value_margin, 1.0 + args.value_margin\n        ret_clipped = ((ret_d < _lo) | (ret_d > _hi)).mean()\n            \"n_terminal\": n_terminal,\n            \"seat_sum\": seat_sum_mean, \"seat_sum_absdev\": seat_sum_absdev,\n            \"value_mean\": flatten(traj[\"value\"]).mean(),\n            \"rewarded_frac\": rewarded_frac, \"ev\": ev_d, \"ev_signal\": ev_sig_d,\n            \"ret_mean\": ret_d.mean(), \"ret_std\": ret_d.std(),\n            # **Is the shaping doing anything, and is the critic able to represent it.**\n            #\n            # `shape_share` is the share of total absolute reward that came from shaping rather than\n            # from the win/loss. Near 0 means the coefficient is too small to matter; near 1 means it\n            # has become the objective, which invariance does NOT protect against -- the theorem\n            # fixes the optimum, not what PPO does with clipping and finite samples.\n            #\n            # `ret_clipped` is the fraction of GAE returns falling outside `[-margin, 1 + margin]`,\n            # i.e. outside what the sigmoid head can express. **This is the important one.** A clipped\n            # critic looks exactly like a confident one: `explained_variance` stays high because the\n            # predictions are self-consistent, and the error is invisible. The correct fix is book-cost valuation in two symmetric entries (held seeds AND standing crops at SEED_COST), it is trivially policy-invariant, and it is worthless unless Q1 is fixed first.**\n\n## Question 1 — the scale\n\n**The numbers.** PHI'(m) = 2m/SCALE² = 2m/10¹⁰ per dollar:\n\n| net worth | PHI | marginal PHI per $1 | $250 harvest moves PHI by |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| $3,000 (where the policy is) | 0.0009 | 6.0e-7 | 1.56e-4 (1 : 6,400 of a win) |\n| $30,000 | 0.09 | 6.0e-6 | 1.5e-3 |\n| $100,000 | 1.0 | 2.0e-5 | 5.0e-3 |\n\nThe policy's measured envelope is bank median $0, max $85, so its whole lived experience maps into PHI ∈ [0, ~0.001] — **0.1% of the potential's range**. The telescoped total shaping for an entire season, PHI(end) − PHI(start), is at most ~0.001 against a terminal win of 1.0. Advantage normalization does not rescue this: `scripts/train_jax_rl.py:1251-1253` divides shaping rows and terminal rows by the *same* batch std, which is set by the ±1 terminal spikes (present in every batch — the policy wins 0.621 vs pool), so the relative 1:6,400 survives normalization intact.\n\n**You can confirm this for free, right now, without touching the run.** `shape_share` is already logged every update (`scripts/train_jax_rl.py:2413-2419`, emitted at 2679-2682), and the code's own comment says \"Near 0 means the coefficient is too small to matter.\" Prediction: the running `shaped` job is reporting shape_share ~1e-3 or less. Read the log before doing anything else.\n\n**Is the convexity load-bearing? No, and the file's own argument proves it.** The Elo/margin justification (`kagg/rl/money_potential.py:10-14`) defends the potential *form* F = γΦ(s′) − Φ(s) against adding f(final_bank) to the terminal reward. That argument is correct and says nothing about EXPONENT — *any* Φ is policy-invariant; the exponent only chooses *where* credit is relocated, and 2.0 relocates it to the $30k–$100k band, which this policy has never once visited. The design requirement \"$5 over an equally broke mirror is worth almost nothing\" is satisfied by SCALE alone: under linear Φ, $5 is worth 5e-5. The one job convexity genuinely took over after FLOOR moved to 0 (`money_potential.py:44-55`) — making stake-clawback worthless — is also mostly SCALE's doing: linear thrift on the full $3,000 stake caps at 0.03·coef, and since episodes *start* at $3,000 (`kagg/sim/jax_sim.py:487`, `starting_money=3000.0`) the telescoping means holding the stake pays exactly 0; only going broke moves Φ, negatively — which, for a policy documented as \"bankrupt every game,\" is a gradient you *want*.\n\n**Ranked responses:**\n1. **EXPONENT 2.0 -> 1.0, keep SCALE 1e5, keep coef 1.0.** The recommended fix. Gradient at $3k multiplies by SCALE/2m ≈ 16.7x (more below $3k). A $250 harvest becomes 2.5e-3 (1:400), a teacher-grade $28k season telescopes to 0.28 — real credit on the turns earnings happen. `--value-margin 0.3` is then *exactly* at the boundary (season shaping can reach coef·CAP = 1.0 only near $100k; at realistic reachability, 0.28); watch `ret_clipped` (`train_jax_rl.py:2684-2687`) — any non-zero reading means raise the margin.\n2. **Raise coef instead: dead end.** Making the $250 harvest worth 1% of a win needs coef ≈ 64; a season that later reaches PHI ≈ 1 then contributes 64 vs terminal 1 — the exact failure the CAP comment records at coef 6 contributing 7.3 (`money_potential.py:57-62`), and the sigmoid value head (`kagg/rl/jax_policy.py:170-172, 279-289`) cannot represent it at any sane margin.\n3. **EXPONENT 0.5 (sqrt): don't.** Maximum low-end gradient ($250 -> 7.1e-3) but PHI(3000) = 0.173, so full-stake clawback pays 0.17·coef — this resurrects Alex's original thrift objection for real.\n4. **Shrink SCALE with EXPONENT 2 (e.g. 10k): worse than linear.** Gradient appears at $3k but the CAP flattens everything above $10k — flat precisely where you want the policy to go next.\n\nOne honesty note: `money_potential.py:36-38` warns that switching shaping mid-stream makes runs incomparable. Changing EXPONENT mid-run compounds that. The clean move is a restart from the same DAgger init with linear Φ — the init is cheap to reload and the current shaped run is, per the arithmetic above, approximately an unshaped run anyway.\n\n## Question 2 — \"plant should be zero\"\n\n**The diagnosis correction, with citations.** PLANT does not spend cash. Cash leaves at ORDER_BUY_SEED in the orders pass — `kagg/sim/jax_sim.py:1022` charges `SEED_COST`, and line 1045 books the purchase into `farm.seeds` (`jax_sim.py:220`), which the code itself notes \"live outside the shed\" (`jax_sim.py:1041`). `net_worth()` (`kagg/rl/net_worth.py:143-152`) sums money + livestock + stock + standing yield; `stock_value` (`net_worth.py:92-107`) reads only `shed + carried` on the PRODUCTS+ANIMALS axis. **`farm.seeds` appears nowhere in `net_worth.py`** — I grepped; a held seed is marked at $0. So:\n\n- BUY_SEED: cash −$10..−$100 (kg.CROPS: wheat 10, carrot 20, tomato 50, melon 80, strawberry 100), asset +$0 -> **dPHI < 0**. This is where the asymmetry with BUY_ANIMAL (a wash, `net_worth.py:99-106`) actually lives.\n- PLANT (`jax_sim.py:759-782`): converts a $0-book seed into a $0-book standing crop -> **dPHI = 0 already**. Alex's \"no, plant should be zero\" is literally already true.\n\n**The correct fix is two entries, both at book cost, mirroring the animal treatment exactly:**\n1. Value `farm.seeds` at SEED_COST -> BUY_SEED becomes a wash — the precise analogue of \"An animal in the shed is not yet on a feeding clock, so it marks at full cost -- which is what makes buying one a wash\" (`net_worth.py:99-101`).\n2. Value a standing crop at SEED_COST while `kind == PLANT` -> PLANT stays a wash (−seed from the seeds term, +seed on the tile).\n\nDoing only #2 without #1 makes PLANT dPHI = **+seed_cost** — a bribe to plant, which is encoding strategy, the exact thing `net_worth.py:10-13` refuses to do, and CLAUDE.md's raw-PLANT correlation of −0.358 says bribing PLANT per se is actively wrong. Book cost (not expected yield) is correct by this module's own stated criterion: it is a simulator-supplied number (`kagg/sim/farm.py:29`), symmetric with `ANIMAL_COST`, no judgement smuggled in — unlike expected-yield valuation, which `net_worth.py:47-49` and `:124-126` explicitly refuse.\n\n**Policy invariance: trivially yes.** Ng–Harada–Russell holds for *any* Φ(s); whether the valuation is \"correct\" accounting is irrelevant to invariance. The only seam — terminal reset discontinuity — is already handled by the suppression at `scripts/train_jax_rl.py:681-687`.\n\n**Interactions, each checked:**\n- **FERTILIZE hole: none.** `standing_yield_value` (`net_worth.py:110-140`) values grown `yield_units`; book cost values the plant itself. Additive, no double count: HARVEST decrements `yield_units` (`jax_sim.py:1048`) and adds product to the shed — a wash between the two *existing* terms; the book value doesn't move. FERTILIZE still pays via doubled yield appearing in `yield_units`.\n- **Crop death: automatic write-off, and it is a feature.** Two unwatered days sets `kind = WEED, crop = NO_CROP, yield_units = 0` (`jax_sim.py:1246-1250`); a book value gated on `kind == PLANT` vanishes on that turn -> dPHI = −seed_cost exactly when the crop dies. Letting a crop die now costs something on the turn it happens.\n- **`max_lifespan_step`: same path.** End-of-life decay sheds a unit every other step past the boundary and converts to WEED when empty (`jax_sim.py:1211-1225`, dead->WEED at 1222; lifespan set at 1271, `TURNS_PER_DAY = 24` at `kagg/sim/tiles.py:25`). Automatic write-off. Minor wart: the plant marks at full seed cost while its yield decays — same class of conservatism already accepted for price impact (`net_worth.py:51-54`). At $10–$100, ignorable.\n- **DIG** (`jax_sim.py:722-728`): digging a live plant books −seed_cost. Correct — the asset was destroyed.\n- Seeds never decay, so hoarding bought seeds is a permanent wash. Harmless.\n\n**What must be true for it to be measurably better — and the blunt part.** Under the *current* squared potential, the entire dispute is about Φ′(3000)·$80 ≈ 4.8e-5 per melon seed — 1:20,000 of a win. Fixing the sign of a 5e-5 signal changes nothing; **this fix is only worth doing together with the Q1 rescale.** Under linear Φ the BUY_SEED penalty is 8e-4·coef per melon seed, ~0.016·coef of spurious negative credit across a 20-plant season — and it lands *inside* the ~20-turn credit window while the harvest payoff lands 48–240 turns out (first_yield_day 2–10 days x 24 turns/day), outside `--horizon 32` for everything but wheat and carrot. That mechanism — penalty inside the window, payoff outside — must be what suppresses planting for the fix to matter, on a policy already at 8.7% plant and ratio 0.37 vs the teacher's 2.05.\n\n**Cheapest experiments, in order:**\n1. **Free, zero runs:** read `shape_share` and `ret_clipped` from the current run's log (`train_jax_rl.py:2419, 2684`). shape_share ~1e-3 confirms Q1 empirically and confirms the current `shaped` run is burning GPU on a flag that does nothing.\n2. **No training:** replay a few recorded teacher seasons through `net_worth()` with and without the seed/book-cost terms and histogram per-op dPHI. Confirms BUY_SEED goes −80 -> 0, PLANT stays 0 -> 0, death books −80, and *nothing else moves* — catches any double-count before GPU time.\n3. **One A/B, ~50 updates:** same DAgger init, linear Φ, coef 1.0, with vs without the book-cost terms. Success = harvest/plant ratio moving from 0.37 toward 2.05 and vs-farm coming off 0.000/221.
